# LYNDRY cleanup audit — 26 September 2026

Read-only audit of the `dev` workspace (`C:\Users\neil\Desktop\LYNDRY-dev`, HEAD `cb671ca`). **Nothing in this document has been changed in the code yet.**

How it was done: eighteen auditors, one per area, then every deletion candidate was given to two independent reviewers whose job was to prove it was still in use. It survives as a deletion only if both failed. A final critic audited the ten source files no auditor had cited.

## The one fact that decides how aggressive this can be

**Production (`main`) runs the van model and has no courier code at all** — no `quote.js`, `shop.js`, `courier-legs.js` or Uber provider. Everything van/driver, and the old `/o/<code>` laundromat pages that real partners and printed QR stickers use, is the *live business*. So van-only code is classified **LEGACY** (isolate later), never DELETE, unless it is dead in *both* models.

| | |
|---|---|
| Inventory items | 720 |
| KEEP / REPLACE / LEGACY / DELETE / UNKNOWN | 306 / 86 / 109 / 138 / 81 |
| Deletion candidates put to two reviewers | 129 |
| **Confirmed dead by both reviewers** | **105** (100 low risk, 5 medium) |
| Refuted — still in use, kept | 24 |
| Lines in the confirmed-dead set | **~3,679** measured (the auditors' own estimates sum to 7,577, inflated by overlapping findings) |
| Business-rule conflicts needing your decision | 152 |
| Stale copy | 502 (161 customer-facing) |

## Defects found along the way (not deletions — these are bugs)

- **sendAndLog carrier-failure path** (`src/core/notify.js:286-311`) — When Telnyx refuses a text after its retries (telnyx.js:233, 247 throw), notify catches the error and still inserts an OUTBOUND messages row with provider_message_id null. It then returns undefined, which is exactly what a success returns. Callers treat the failure as sent: card-chase.js:154-172 and payment-chase.js:136 stamp the chase as done, and intake shows 'Asked ..., awaiting reply'. alreadySaid() (notify.js:187) then matches the failed row, so a retry within 30 seconds is refused as a duplicate. This contradicts the rule in notify.js:238-240 and message-view.js:9-13 that `messages` is 'the record of what actually reached a phone'.
- **Team-phone set omits SUPPORT_PHONE** (`src/routes/admin.js:9012-9013, 9385, 9536; src/core/issues.js:44`) — alertRecipients() always adds config.supportPhone, but teamPhoneSet is built only from ops_users.phone. If Neil's SUPPORT_PHONE has no ops_users row (order-alerts.js:51-55 explicitly allows this), every new-order, paused-AI, bag-ready and issue page sent to it shows up in /ops/messages as a real stranger's conversation instead of sorting as ops noise. config.supportPhone is also not normalised (config.js:235), so a dashed value will never match either set. Fix: add normalisePhone(config.supportPhone) to the set.

Other defects the area auditors reported are inside their summaries below — notably: a guest whose number already belongs to a customer is signed in **without a code** and overwrites that account (account-auth); the `/ops/weights` form renders its values literally so all five inputs show empty, also on production (ops-routes); `stripe.refund()` is unreachable because of a wiring bug (payments); the thank-you page shows the one-time rate on subscription orders (account-auth).

## 4. Area summaries

### public-site
KEEP 17 · REPLACE 3 · LEGACY 5 · DELETE 7 · UNKNOWN 3

The public site is live and wired correctly: every page in PAGES is in the nav or footer and the sitemap, and the special routes (llms.txt, robots, sitemap, the ads CSV, /signup→/account/login, /p/, /pay/, /for-laundromats/<token>) all have a real caller. What doesn't hold together is the content. Today's hero rewrite and the Lyn-cannot-book decision left the rest of the site behind. Dead leftovers of the old hero: textUsQrSvg/QR_SVG, the START_PROBLEM banner (POST /start still redirects bad input to a home page that can no longer show it), five unused site tokens and ICON_PHONE, and the .order-online CSS rule. These are safe DELETEs, about 150 lines in total. Only /bergen and the offer popup still capture phone numbers, and they drop people into a text thread whose pages and first message invite a booking Lyn must refuse. That funnel needs a business decision, as does whether /bergen still gets any ad traffic. Texting is still presented as the way to order on Contact, the terms, the carrier-reviewed sms-terms page (which also says opt-in happens on the home page, where no phone box exists now), llms.txt, schema.js, the vCard, all 71 location pages and a dozen town leads. The copy telling people we text them a link to save a card is also stale now that the payment method is taken inside the online order form. The biggest problem is pricing: /quote and /pricing (courier-only, and broken under the FLAT model production runs) show a per-address rate, while every order is billed at the flat $2.00/$1.80. The quote route also uses its own superseded 10-mile rule with no New Jersey check instead of core/serviceable.js. Bergen County stays hard-coded in titles, JSON-LD, the hub and the default share image, which also advertises '$2.00 a pound' and 'TEXT' to order. CLAUDE.md and site.js disagree about whether napiii LLC exists.

### account-auth
KEEP 25 · REPLACE 3 · LEGACY 0 · DELETE 7 · UNKNOWN 4

The account area is live in production. main's account.js differs from dev only in the out-of-area wording and the ZIP check, and booking-intents, card-saved, customer-auth and account-setup are identical on both branches. It is now the only way to place an order. The auth flow end to end: POST /account/login normalises the number and looks up customers.phone (account.js:351). - An UNSUBSCRIBED number is refused. - An existing customer gets a 6-digit HMAC-hashed code. It is stored in customer_login_codes (5-minute TTL, 5 attempts) and texted after 3 seconds via code-sender, with an unsigned ly_cust_pending cookie. - The customer taps Sign in (sign-in-tap), verifyCode runs, and the ly_cust cookie is set: id.expiry.HMAC, SameSite=Lax, httpOnly, 14 days, path /account. requireCustomer re-reads the row on every request. - A new number gets no text. It gets a signed Strict 60-minute ly_guest cookie and walks wash → repeat → when → address. At the address step the consent tick triggers onboarding.startConversation, the only customers insert, and a session. - With a card on file, the order is booked, texted, and ends on /account/thanks. - Without one, a booking intent is saved, POST /account/card goes to Stripe, and /account/card/done (or the webhook, or /pay/<token>/done) converts the intent into the order. For email+password: the phone-as-only-credential entry (phoneStep and the existing-customer code branch as the default) is REPLACE. The session cookie, requireCustomer, the code path (SMS fallback, recovery, phone verification before attaching an email), the guest cookie, the consent tick and every operational text keyed on customers.phone all stay. Email should be attached to the existing customers row. customers.email already exists but is unverified, non-unique and filled only by Lyn. Do it inside a phone-verified session, never by inserting a new row (phone is NOT NULL UNIQUE). Van assumptions leak into shared paths in five places. Every booking plans its laundromat with the van chooser (dispatch.savePlannedPartner). orders.create auto-assigns a driver. dispatch.js hosts payment gates that courier code imports. The run and board ignore carriers.js, so in-house legs and courier legs are not told apart. And /ops/run, /ops/clips, /ops/loadout and the crew strip are not model-gated. Recommended boundary: move the van-only code into src/core/van and src/web/van (run, board and sequencing, clips, clip stock, the van fulfilment steps). Pull the payment gates and bag-tag identity out as shared modules. Pick the laundromat chooser and driver assignment per model. Replace the model-only vanOnly check with a carrier-aware gate, carriers.isDriver(order, leg), used both to filter the board and to guard the pages.

The laundromat area has two parallel front doors with little overlap. /o/<code> (bag.js) is production's van workflow, and printed stickers point at it. Before the portal replaces it, Neil needs to decide how a portal order becomes READY and records its bag count, whether weigh-first gating and per-bag weights apply, and which tolerance flags a mismatch. ### payments
KEEP 16 · REPLACE 0 · LEGACY 5 · DELETE 7 · UNKNOWN 6

Four bugs in the charge paths are live or about to ship, and they matter more than the dead code. (1) Production, both branches: the Stripe webhook (src/routes/payments.js:255-301) treats $25 show-up holds as the order's charge. Capturing a hold can overwrite a just-written FAILED with PAID, which clears the payment hold, and the remainder is never collected. A refused hold stamps an uncollected order FAILED. (2) Production: the new "another bag turned up" charge calls chargeOrder on a PAID order. chargeOrder silently does nothing, yet the customer is texted "We have taken the extra $X". (3) Dev only, from c6f4665: settleTotal decides trip-vs-wash money by IN_OUR_HANDS. At a van door the order is already IN_PROCESS, so the kept $25 is recorded as wash money and recordShowUp becomes unreachable. (4) Both branches: stripe.js turns every Stripe error, including key or configuration errors, into an ordinary result the code reads as a card decline. At a van door that means bags left on the step with a "declined" text. Lyn can also still book, and place holds, through save_details' pickup_date, despite CANNOT_BOOK. In the rest of the area, the core pieces are sound and used by both models: the ledger, cash recovery, the hold lifecycle (booking hold, night-before refresh, release on cancel), and card-saved as the single "a card was saved" path. The van door charge (loadVan, declinedAtTheDoor), deliver()'s charge-and-deliver-anyway backstop, payment-chase, recordShowUp and extra-bag are van-only LEGACY. Provably dead in both models: refundDeposit (it can only no-op or throw, because payments.refund was never exported), stripe.refund, payments.forOrders, GET /account/booked/:token with bookedPage, GET /account/payment, one-off scripts/book-francisco.js, and a few unused requires, about 450 lines in all. The parked inline card-field code and the deposit_* fallbacks need a decision from you or one SQL check first. The copy is the biggest mess. It still teaches "deliver and chase" in six places against the 14 Sep payment-hold rule. It says "charged once" where the consent text describes two charges, and "at your door" where the courier model weighs at the laundromat; the Stripe consent text itself is van-only. "Text us to book" is now wrong after today's Lyn decision, and "card" and "payment method" are used interchangeably. The margins and order minimums were not touched.

### exports-core
KEEP 4 · REPLACE 0 · LEGACY 3 · DELETE 15 · UNKNOWN 5

I checked every src/core entry from deadexports.py by hand, then ran a second pass the textual scan cannot do. That pass follows each module's own import variable, so it catches exports whose names collide with unrelated identifiers. Examples: tags.claim, tags.total, bags.pickupBags and core/payments.forOrders were missed because other code has its own `claim`, `.total`, `stop.pickupBags` and `bags.forOrders`. It also shows that describeChoices and netCents only looked used inside their own files because a comment mentions them. Most of the ~125 src/core entries are fine. They are either used inside their own module, so the only fix is to take them off module.exports, or they are small pure rules that a test pins on purpose. About 20 functions are really dead: no caller on dev or on main, so dead in both models including production van. Most are van or order-tag leftovers from deletions that took the route but left the core function behind. For example, bags.unloadBags lost its caller in bbc9fe6, nextSteps in cbaa1df, and withinReachOf in 8d0ae55. Removing the DELETE items saves roughly 390 lines of src/core, plus test edits for withinReachOf, spot-photo.has, orderRateLine and cancellationFeeCents. Four more are dead but need a decision from Neil before removal: courier sendForPickup (built, never wired), orders.reinstate (no button), quote.netCents (no margin check uses it) and billing.createInlineCardSetup (parked, and not actually tested). The merged-branch deletions are confirmed gone. workCard, pickupSequence and correctionLabel survive only in comments (admin.js:59, 3418, 3436) and in stale TASKS.md:111-114. None of the four /ops/run sub-routes is mounted any more. The most important findings are not dead code. They are two ways the "Lyn cannot book" decision from 26 Sep is broken in src/core: 1. save_details is still offered to the model, still accepts pickup_date, and calls createOrder directly (actions.js:897-907), which gets around the CANNOT_BOOK gate. 2. brain.decide still forces tool_choice to check_slot, which is no longer in the tool list. The Anthropic API is expected to reject that call with a 400. Every inbound text containing "now", "today", "tomorrow", a weekday or a time would then fail. sms.js swallows the error, raises an "AI could not be reached" issue and replies with nothing. The tests never call the API, so they cannot catch this. Main is not affected; dev would be on release. Several customer-facing texts in onboarding.js, intake.js and actions.js still say "say when and I'll book it". Out of scope but listed by the scan: the src/web, src/providers and src/routes entries (order-console, shop-page, schema, telnyx, couriers/fake). I did not verify those.

### docs
KEEP 6 · REPLACE 14 · LEGACY 4 · DELETE 8 · UNKNOWN 3

The eight root docs add up to about 7,700 lines. CLAUDE.md is 6,071 of them (about 62k words, roughly 85k tokens read at every session start), and it mixes current rules with dated incident narrative: 44 order-number incidents and about 38 'used to / superseded' passages. Its headings no longer match what is under them: the 'Leads off the Facebook adverts' section swallows 1,268 lines of unrelated topics. The most important fact, two operating models switched by PRICING_MODEL with production on the van and courier code only on dev, appears once, at line 5321. `npm run test:both` and the config margins are not documented anywhere. Only CLAUDE.md differs between main and dev (+518 courier lines). Most of the other docs are stale: - **README** (August): says the AI isn't built, lists Tailwind, and still gives a seed command that once overwrote Neil's live row. - **TESTING** (11 Aug) and **ROUTING** (13 Aug): out of date; ROUTING is a van-only design doc. - **TASKS** (2 Sep): mostly false now. - **HANDOFF**: its work shipped to main on 15 Sep. - **DECISIONS**: carries about 20 reversed entries, while AGENTS.md tells agents never to simplify it. Today's 'Lyn cannot book' decision is on dev only; main still books by text. On dev, CLAUDE.md, Lyn's own prompt (the setup beats, confirm-before-booking, set_pickup_schedule), the first message ('Want us to pick up your laundry?'), /contact and the /bergen advert funnel all still invite booking by text. The post-delivery subscription text asks for a reply Lyn can no longer act on. Under the courier model, the prompt also types a '$25 minimum' five times (the courier minimum is $45) and says 'no delivery fee' when a fee is charged. Proposed restructure, done on dev: - **Root:** a rewritten README, a CLAUDE.md of about 1,000-1,200 lines of active rules (opening with the two-models / two-databases / test:both block, then non-negotiables, conventions, an accurate file map, per-channel and per-model money and facts, SMS compliance, website, ops and roles, dev environment), a dated DECISIONS log with a status on each entry, and AGENTS.md only if Neil still uses that workflow. - **docs/van-model.md** (LEGACY): run, clips, load-out, door charge, dispatch. - **docs/laundromat-tag-flow.md** (LEGACY): /o/<code> and /processing, still used by the production laundromats. - **docs/courier-model.md:** the Uber findings, the portal and the courier hold, marked dev-only. - **docs/lyn-booking-by-text.md** (LEGACY): live on main, dormant on dev. - **docs/design/van-routing.md:** the current ROUTING.md. - **docs/testing-sms.md:** the rewritten TESTING.md. - **docs/history/:** incidents, reversed rules, TASKS, HANDOFF, the $39-bag and design-handoff notes. Constraints on the restructure: - test/booking-intent.test.js:507-517 requires the literal 'AN ONLINE ORDER IS NOT WRITTEN UNTIL A CARD IS SAVED' in CLAUDE.md. - 119 code comments in 77 files cite CLAUDE.md rules by name, so keep section names stable or leave 'moved to' stubs. - home.html, bergen.html and vcard.js cite DECISIONS.md. - scripts/seed-week.js:20,451 cites ROUTING.md section 6.

### tests
KEEP 81 · REPLACE 8 · LEGACY 22 · DELETE 6 · UNKNOWN 5

test/ has 101 files and about 20,100 lines. `grep -c test(` finds about 1,327 declarations; the HEAD commit reports 1331 passing. I did not run the suite. Every test file loads a module that still exists. The only missing files tests mention are the deliberately absent nudges.js and nudge-panel.js (the tombstone test in intake.test.js). Nothing in test/ refers to the already-deleted workCard, pickupSequence, correctionLabel or the removed /ops/run sub-routes. The only /ops/run paths the tests use, /ops/run and /ops/run/handed-off, are still mounted (admin.js:6039, 6759). No test pins the removed home-page hero form or phone mock: consent.test.js dropped the hero today, and only popup.js posts to /start now. By model: 15 files exist only on dev and cover courier or partner-portal code (carriers, courier-*, couriers-board, uber-response-shape, pickup-leg, weigh-in-charge, laundromat-portal, partner-*, service-area, settle-weight-fields, order-event-kinds, no-control-characters). About 17 files cover van-only or old-laundromat features that production still runs. I classed those LEGACY, not DELETE. The main structural weakness: 74 of 101 files read source code as text with readFileSync and regex, slicing function bodies by indexOf. This contradicts CLAUDE.md:114-116 ('the tests cover the pure functions') and CLAUDE.md:3923 ('nothing else in test/ does'). It makes the suite brittle to refactors, and a few tests assert on comments or CLAUDE.md prose. The biggest problem is booking-by-text. Tests in booking-flow-order, wash-after-booking, lyn-locked-rules, weekday, intake and silent-handoff still pin the booking-by-text machinery that CANNOT_BOOK was meant to switch off. wash-after-booking.test.js:48 even pins save_details' createOrder path, which gets around CANNOT_BOOK entirely. No test checks what actually happens when Lyn tries to book: that actions.run refuses the tool, that save_details with a date does not book, or that the forced tool_choice in brain.decide names a tool the model is offered. That last gap hides a likely dev-only outage. Any text naming a day or time ('Friday', 'tomorrow', 'now') forces check_slot, which the model is no longer offered. The API is expected to reject that call; the thread is then paused and admins are paged. Other gaps: compliance.js (STOP/HELP), customer-auth.js, throttle.js and weight.js have no tests. Six small test-level deletions are recommended: exact duplicates in still-photo-scan, custody-not-the-van and reminder-collectable, the CLAUDE.md and comment assertions, and one tombstone. They total about 97 lines. Everything larger is flagged update, keep-as-legacy or needs a decision, per the flip-flop guidance.

### schema
KEEP 6 · REPLACE 1 · LEGACY 9 · DELETE 2 · UNKNOWN 9

The schema is 107 migrations. 0001-0100 are on main and in production; 0101-0107 (partner portal, courier legs, carrier override) exist only on dev. All 32 tables are still referenced by code. I checked in both directions: every column the code filters on, selects, writes or reads off a row object, against every column any migration creates. Three columns are used but never created by a migration: partner_users.last_seen_at (dev only; it breaks listing, adding and editing laundromat staff) and app_settings.weight_dry_loss_pct and weight_gain_lb. The last two have been in production code since 2 Sep; reads fall back to defaults, but saving /ops/admin/weights fails on any database built from migrations. The backup script also silently skips the payments ledger, booking_intents and lead_outreach, and restore can never complete because three tables have no id column. In the other direction, the dead or write-only schema is small and well contained: customers.recurring_* (fully superseded), promotions.auto_grant (write-only), deposit_* (still read for two historic orders), the shelved locker model (lockers, buildings, locker_id, ASSIGNED/DEPOSITED), bag_labels.intended_partner_id/partner_locked and orders.intended_partner_at (write-only), orders.tag_code (never written by any commit), vehicles.clip_count, a half-built partner-invoicing set (partner_bill_*, weight_band, billing terms) whose pay rule contradicts reports.js, and in the unreleased chain delivery_fee_cents, courier_cost_cents and courier_deliveries.quote_id, which nothing writes. I am not recommending any drops. Each of those needs a data check or a business decision first, and then a later migration. The only schema change that is safe now is additive (add the two missing app_settings columns with 'if not exists'), plus a one-word code fix for last_seen_at. Two code-only deletions are safe on both models: tags.claim/tagUrl and bag.js weightCard, about 170 lines. Stale comments inside already-applied migrations should be corrected in CLAUDE.md or the next migration's header, because scripts/migrate.js checksums every applied file.

### critic (the ten uncovered files)

I audited all ten uncited files on dev; all ten are identical on main. None is DELETE. notify.js, roles.js, db.js, order-events.js, message-view.js, pitch-link.js, intake-table.js, format.js and phone.normalisePhone are KEEP, and every export has a live caller except these hygiene items: order-alerts.compose and PERMISSION are unused outside their file (un-export); isFictional, message-view.digits and format.NO_DATE were already listed by exports-core; order-events.sameAs and SAME_TAP_SECONDS are test seams (test/one-event-per-tap.test.js); pitch-link.mint is a test seam (test/pitch-link.test.js). phone.js is not a leftover: it owns the stored +1 format and format.js owns display. Its one leftover is formatPhone, a pass-through to format.displayPhone with 35 references including three scripts (REPLACE). New findings no auditor made: (1) production new-order alerts go to every orders.view holder, which includes all drivers, and carry the customer's name and card state, against roles.js's driver rule; (2) notify.js logs a carrier-refused text as a normal OUTBOUND row and returns the same as success, so chases stamp it as done and the 30-second duplicate check blocks the retry; (3) SUPPORT_PHONE is added to every alert list but is left out of message-view's team set and is not normalised; (4) login codes bypass notify, so the 555-01XX fiction guard does not cover them; (5) pitch-link, bags (production stickers) and /health compare signatures in a way that can throw and return a 500; (6) SERVICE_TZ is declared in three places and there is a second text-date formatter in onboarding.expiryNote. I listed six stale comments, all internal. The thinnest categories across the audit are authentication to be replaced (ADMIN_API_KEY is both the all-powerful machine key and the HMAC root for sessions, stickers and pitch links), duplicate business logic in shared plumbing, duplicate order-placing and chase workflows, and an explicit never-remove list.

**Thin coverage across the whole audit:**

Judged from the 18 area summaries and the 702/148/496 inventory/conflict/staleCopy entries: WELL COVERED (no action): unused pages/routes (ops-routes 142 routes, public-site); unused functions/modules (exports-core, exports-web-css; the ten plumbing files above were the gap and are now done); stale customer copy (161 customer-facing entries); stale docs (docs area, CLAUDE.md); one-off scripts (33 scripts entries, including book-francisco and check-thread); van-only functionality (van-driver, the vanOnly gate); old laundromat workflows (laundromat area, /o/ vs /shop). THIN: (1) AUTHENTICATION TO BE REPLACED. Only customer auth got a flow review. Nobody looked at ADMIN_API_KEY being both the bearer machine credential (x-admin-key gets everything through roles.can isMachine, roles.js:147; admin-auth.js:307; index.js:178-182) and the HMAC root for staff sessions (admin-auth.js:70), customer sessions (customer-auth.js:43), partner sessions (partner-auth.js:76), printed bag stickers (bags.js:147) and pitch links (pitch-link.js:39). Rotating it after a leak would kill every sticker in the field and sign everybody out ('isMachine' has 0 hits in audit.json and 'rotat' has none in this sense). Also unreviewed: the three parallel SMS sign-in stacks. Compare admin-auth.js, customer-auth.js and partner-auth.js for duplicated cookie, sameSecret and code-sender set-up, and decide whether one module should own them. (2) DUPLICATE BUSINESS LOGIC in shared plumbing. Covered for money (about 16 formatters) and addresses. Not covered: SERVICE_TZ in 3 places; the 8 constant-time compare copies (3 length-unsafe); the phone-digit helpers; normalisePhone copied into web.js; the two text-date formatters (booking.readableDate, onboarding.expiryNote). (3) DUPLICATE WORKFLOWS. Only /o/ vs /shop and the pricing split were compared. Not compared: the order-placing doors (account wizard + booking-intents.convert, admin.js:4227 and 5073 ops booking, recurring.js:237/406/530, and actions.js:79 create_order, withheld on dev but live on main) for which rules each re-runs; and the three card and payment chasers (card-chase.js, payment-chase.js, reminders.js card ask) for overlapping texts to the same customer. (4) HIGH-RISK REMOVALS. There are 41 high-risk items, but nobody wrote an explicit never-remove list. Record notify.js, roles.js, db.js, order-events.js, compliance.js, the provider_message_id dedupe (sms.js:40-58) and the Stripe/Telnyx webhook routes as must-preserve, each with its guarding test (which-database.test.js, lyn-locked-rules.test.js, one-event-per-tap.test.js). (5) UNCLEAR-PURPOSE FUNCTIONALITY. 80 UNKNOWNs are still unresolved. Most are decisions for Neil rather than code questions, so group them by decision owner instead of by area. (6) BUSINESS-RULE CONFLICTS INSIDE CROSS-CUTTING MODULES. Permission leaks by SMS (order-alerts to drivers) and failure semantics (notify logs carrier failures as sent) sit between areas, which is why no single auditor saw them. Check the other alertRecipients callers the same way: paused-alerts.js:136 ('messages.view') and bag.js:1615 ('orders.act', which drivers hold). For each, confirm the body carries nothing the recipient's role may not see.

## 5. Business-rule conflicts needing your decision

None of these has been decided. Each is listed with both sides.

### account-auth
- **Customer password login: ruled out vs. the new direction** — The build plan and the auth module both rule out a customer password login, and the public copy promises there is no password. Today's direction is email+password as the future primary login.
  - *Decision:* Formally lift the CLAUDE.md prohibition now and schedule the copy change for when email login ships, or keep phone+code as the documented rule until then.
  - *Where:* CLAUDE.md:38 ('Explicitly not being built: A customer app or account login'), src/core/customer-auth.js:13-20 ('No password ... deliberately NOT the customer account login the build plan rules out'), public/pages/faq.html:35-36 ('sign in with your mobile number rather than a password to remember'),…
- **SMS consent: 'not a condition of purchase' vs. required to order** — The carrier-registered consent sentence says consent is not a condition of purchase, but the order wizard refuses to book a guest who does not tick it. This becomes sharper once email could carry order messages.
  - *Decision:* Either make the tick optional for web orders (and decide how a non-consenting customer gets confirmations and reminders), or keep it required and have the consent wording reviewed, since it is pinned by test/consent.test.js and was shown to carriers.
  - *Where:* src/routes/account.js:1952-1953 and src/web/site.js:119 ('Consent is not a condition of purchase.'), src/routes/account.js:1948 (checkbox `required`), src/routes/account.js:2656-2661 ('LYNDRY runs on text, so we cannot book a pickup without it.')
- **Texting STOP locks a customer out of the website** — Status UNSUBSCRIBED blocks sign-in and ends an existing session, because today's login is a text. With a password login, STOP (which only governs texting) would still lock people out of ordering online.
  - *Decision:* Once a non-SMS login exists, may an opted-out customer sign in and place orders, and if so, how are they told about pickups?
  - *Where:* src/core/customer-auth.js:262, 306 (no code for non-ACTIVE), src/core/customer-auth.js:364, 392-395 (attachCustomer/requireCustomer reject non-ACTIVE and clear the session), src/routes/account.js:363-368 ('You asked us to stop texting this number ... Text START')
- **New numbers are never verified, yet get a consent record and a session** — By design a new number gets no text and goes straight into the order. At the address step a customer row, legal consent (timestamp and IP) and a session are all created on an unverified number. When startConversation finds the number already exists (created:false), the code signs the guest into that account and overwrites its wash and address. The code's own earlier rule forbids exactly that.
  - *Decision:* Require a texted code before creating the account (or at least whenever the number already exists), or accept the zero-friction flow and its risk. The email migration should attach email only to a phone-verified row either way.
  - *Where:* src/routes/account.js:383-391 (new number: no text, guest cookie), src/core/customer-auth.js:139-143 ('nobody has verified they hold that phone'), src/core/onboarding.js:519-523 and src/routes/account.js:2669-2676 (consent recorded), src/routes/account.js:2694, 2703-2708 (session set and record ove…
- **Courier model: address-based quote vs. flat rates in the wizard and on the order** — Under PRICING_MODEL=DYNAMIC, /quote prices by address: the laundromat's rate grossed up per category, plus a pass-through delivery fee. Its result page sends people to /account/login. There the wizard shows the flat $2.00/$1.80 and the minimum with no delivery fee, and bookPickup snapshots config.pricing.perPoundCents onto the order. This does not affect production (van) today.
  - *Decision:* Under the courier model, does the wizard show and charge the address quote (and store the delivery fee on the order), or does /quote fall back to the flat rate? The margins in config.js stay as they are either way.
  - *Where:* src/routes/web.js:146-151 and src/core/quote.js:75-111 (quoteFor: per-lb by laundromat + deliveryFeeCents), src/web/quote-result.js:160 (link to /account/login), src/routes/account.js:182-187, 941-943, 1449-1453, 2181-2183, 2308-2323 (flat rates), src/core/subscription.js:80 and src/core/booking.js…
- **Who weighs, and when the card is charged: van copy is hard-coded** — Wizard and card copy promise the laundry is weighed "at your door". Under the courier model nothing of ours touches the bags and the laundromat's scale sets the price.
  - *Decision:* Make this copy depend on the model (config.courier.model), like site.serviceArea already does, before DYNAMIC ships.
  - *Where:* src/routes/account.js:942-943, 1133-1137, 2183, src/web/account-setup.js:300-302, src/routes/payments.js:186, src/core/billing.js:94, 354, CLAUDE.md:4523-4530 (under a courier the laundromat's figure bills)
- **The show-up hold is disclosed in texts and on Stripe but not on the website** — When a card is saved for a pickup within a day, a $25 authorization is placed (under courier, at least the delivery fee). The confirmation text and Stripe's consent text both say so. The website's card step, card button and thank-you page say nothing is charged and never mention a hold. billing.js's own comment says "nothing is charged today" is false for a same-day pickup.
  - *Decision:* Should the website name the hold before the card step and on the thank-you page, the same way the text and the Stripe page do?
  - *Where:* src/routes/account.js:1495 ('Charged today $0.00'), src/routes/account.js:2182 ('Nothing is charged now.'), src/web/account-setup.js:301, 306 ('Nothing is taken now', 'Save a card, $0 today'), src/core/billing.js:80-81, 95-97 (hold disclosed; 'nothing is charged today' called false), src/core/booki…

### admin-internals
- **Recurring pickups 'by text' vs Lyn cannot book** — The ops customer page tells staff that a subscription is 'Changed by texting us, not from here' and that a customer 'can set one up any time by texting'. But set_pickup_schedule, the tool described as 'Set up, change or stop a subscription', is now in CANNOT_BOOK, so a customer who replies 'yes, weekly' cannot be enrolled by Lyn.

The live call only renders the card when weight_held_at is set, which is exactly the case where that branch does not run. The console's exception strip does not detect this state either.

- **When the card counts as 'taken', per model** — Van-model code charges at the door (fulfilment.loadVan), and admin.js's own comment says so. correctionsCard decides 'the card has been taken' by status === 'DELIVERED'. Dead DONE_MESSAGES says both 'charged at the door' and 'Delivered. The card has been charged'. Under the courier model the charge point is not stated anywhere in admin.js.
  - *Decision:* What event marks 'money has moved' in each model, so correction and extra-bag copy can say whether a change will charge anyone (suggested key: payment_status, not status)?
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:839-843, src/routes/admin.js:1285, src/routes/admin.js:1296-1300, src/routes/admin.js:4747, src/routes/admin.js:4753, src/core/fulfilment.js:1200, src/core/fulfilment.js:1798
- **Model gating is one-directional** — vanOnly hides van screens whenever PRICING_MODEL is DYNAMIC. There is no mirror gate, so the courier-only 'Who drives it' card (with a 'Send a courier' button) and its POST /ops/orders/:id/carrier route are live under the van model. That is what production will run if dev merges before the switch.
  - *Decision:* Should courier-only controls be hidden and refused under the van model (a courierOnly gate) before this branch reaches production?
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:1736-1754, src/routes/admin.js:3288-3299, src/routes/admin.js:2690-2730, src/core/carriers.js:51-53

The by-hand doors onto the same work advertise themselves as safe to run at any hour. Both text customers: bookDue sends 'Your usual pickup is tomorrow', and the script also sends every reminder.

  - *Where:* src/core/scheduler.js:56-59, 74-77, src/core/nightly.js:40-45, scripts/cron-recurring.js:46-49 ('SAFE TO RUN TWICE, OR TEN TIMES, OR AT ANY HOUR'), src/routes/ops.js:286-288 ('Safe to run twice, ten times, or at any hour')
- **The 30-minute card chase texts a raw Stripe link to website customers** — billing.js sets the rule that an unprompted text carrying a payment link looks like phishing: website customers must be sent to their /account page, and that governs everything the system sends on its own. The card chase is exactly such a text. For every unfinished web checkout (booking intents are always web) and for web orders, it sends setupLinkMessage(), which embeds a raw Stripe URL. With Lyn no longer booking, nearly every new order is web, so this becomes the common case.
  - *Decision:* Whether the 'no card on file yet' chase follows the door rule (send web customers to lyndry.com/account) or is a deliberate exception that should be written down as one.

- **'Every text that goes out on its own is visible before it lands' vs what /ops/scheduled shows** — Neil's rule, repeated in the follow-up and reminder modules, is that nothing should text a customer at a time nobody could have predicted, and those two modules expose pending lists. The scheduler now sends at least six kinds of unprompted text. /ops/scheduled lists only follow-ups and reminders. Not shown: the card chase, the payment chase, the morning subscription offer, the hold-refused texts from the nightly pass, and the standing-order booking texts.

- **The 2-hour mid-setup chase exists to push text bookings over the line, and Lyn no longer books** — The early follow-up targets customers part-way through setting up by text (no name, address or card), and rule 4's stated purpose is 'to get somebody over the line'. The prompt now says orders are placed online and Lyn cannot book. The chase still asks the model to repeat its last question, which after today would be a push to /account.
  - *Decision:* Keep the early chase (now effectively 'finish ordering online'), drop it and keep only the day-later chase, or turn follow-ups off for booking-related silences.

- **Price quoted on /quote vs price billed** — /quote works out a per-laundromat rate: partner wholesale / (1 - margin - 2.9%), using the margins at config.js:552. With the seeded wholesale rates of 72-130c, /quote shows about $0.94-$1.69/lb one-time while the order bills $2.00. The /pricing page contradicts itself: line 13 says 'The rate is the same everywhere', lines 127-131 say the rate depends on the laundromat. The $45 minimum reasoning in config.js as…

- **Where the courier-model service area boundary is drawn** — There are four different rules. /quote hard-refuses anything more than 10 straight-line miles from a laundromat and never checks NJ. The booking check requires NJ, shortlists within 30 miles and lets Uber decide, falling back to 10 miles only if Uber is unreachable. The ZIP check on the account form passes 15 miles, which is tighter than booking even though its comment says looser. The /quote refusal tells customers 'we work within 10 miles', while booking.serviceAreaWords() says no mileage may…

- **Which laundromat a courier-model order goes to** — There are three choosers with different goals. dispatch.chooseLaundromat uses van detour cost plus wholesale, writes intended_partner_id at booking in both models, and is what the admin label 'the cheapest all in' means. quote.chooseFor ranks by wash plus a grossed-up delivery fee at 20 lb and decides which laundromat's rate /quote shows. Customers no longer pay that fee, so it is not 'cheapest for the customer'. serviceable.reachable picks the cheapest courier leg and the result is thrown away…

The actual Stripe authorization on dev uses holdFor(), which calls quote.holdCents() = max(floor, 2 x dearest courier band), in both models. On main the van hold is showUpCents(). Both are $25 today, but they diverge the day a band exceeds $12.50 a leg, and the van hold becomes coupled to Uber's price table.

- **Drivers receive every new-order alert with the customer's name and payment state** — order-alerts.js:56 sends to alertRecipients('orders.view'), and issues.js:41 filters with roles.can(u, permission). NO CARD YET / Card on file. <ops link>'. roles.js:95-103 says a driver gets only the address: 'Not the customer's name, not their phone ... not the money'. money.view is withheld from dr…

- **A carrier-refused text is logged and reported exactly like a delivered one** — notify.js logs a failed Telnyx send as an ordinary OUTBOUND row and returns undefined, the same as success. The documented rule (notify.js:238-240, message-view.js:9-13) is that the messages table is the record of what reached a phone. Chases stamp themselves as done, intake shows 'asked, awaiting reply', and the 30-second duplicate check blocks the retry.
  - *Decision:* Keep logging failed attempts, but mark them (for example with a status column, or by leaving provider_message_id null and excluding such rows from alreadySaid) and return {sent:false, refused:'carrier'}? Or stop writing a row on failure?

- **notify.js is not the only way out to a phone** — The 'single choke point' holds for conversation texts only. Login codes go straight to sms.sendMessage through code-sender (admin-auth.js:157, customer-auth.js:217, partner-auth.js:149). That is deliberate, because codes are not logged, but it means the 555-01XX fiction guard does not apply: a seeded staff number such as seed-team.js:38 +12015550101 signing in on a dev box with real Telnyx keys would send a code to the carrier. sms.js:643 helpers.notify is a raw sender that nothing reads; sms-a…
  - *Decision:* Move isFictional() down into providers/sms/index.js so it guards every send, and leave the opt-out gate and logging in notify.js?

- **SUPPORT_PHONE's documented role vs what it actually does** — .env.example:56-57 says SUPPORT_PHONE is 'Used only when the AI gives up and a human needs to take over'. In fact issues.alertRecipients adds it to every alert list: new orders, paused AI, bag ready (bag.js:1615), issue pages and the startup alert (index.js:372). message-view does not treat it as a team number, and config.js:235 does not normalise it.
  - *Decision:* Should SUPPORT_PHONE be normalised and counted as a team number? And should the doc be updated to say it receives all operational alerts?
  - *Where:* .env.example:56-58, src/core/issues.js:44, src/config.js:235, src/routes/admin.js:9012-9013

### payments
- **Does a declined card hold up a delivery?** — The 14 Sep payment-hold rule says laundry we hold does not go back until the balance is nothing. Several code paths and pages still teach, or do, 'deliver and chase'. Most importantly, deliver()'s own backstop charge declines and then delivers anyway.
  - *Decision:* When deliver()'s backstop charge declines at the door, should the driver keep the laundry (payment hold) or hand it over and chase? Then align payment-chase and the staff pages with that answer.
  - *Where:* src/core/billing.js:687-711 (the rule: an in-hand decline is a PAYMENT HOLD), src/core/dispatch.js:120-135 (paymentHold), CLAUDE.md:4865-4880; DECISIONS.md:682-690 (the reversal, 14 Sep), src/core/fulfilment.js:966-968, 989 (deliver(): 'A DECLINE DOES NOT STOP THE DELIVERY ... We deliver and chase …
- **At a van door, is the kept $25 a trip charge or wash money? And is a doorstep decline a 'payment hold'?** — The rule is that the $25 kept when the bags are left pays for the trip and is never credit toward a wash. On dev, settleTotal decides this with IN_OUR_HANDS, but a van doorstep order is already IN_PROCESS (it was 'collected' on arrival, before weighing). So the trip charge is booked as wash money and markFailed pages 'we are holding the laundry'. Main books it correctly; markFailed pages wrongly on both branches.
  - *Decision:* What marks 'we have the laundry' for money purposes: van_confirmed_at / the load step, or the IN_PROCESS status set on arrival? The same answer should drive the ledger's trip-vs-wash choice and the payment-hold page.
  - *Where:* src/config.js:661-683; src/core/payments.js:253-262; src/core/billing.js:883-906 (the rule), src/core/billing.js:1311-1347 (dev custody test, c6f4665), src/core/billing.js:837-856 (markFailed's comment says a doorstep is not a hold; its code pages because IN_PROCESS is in IN_OUR_HANDS), src/core/di…
- **The Stripe webhook treats show-up holds as the order's charge** — Holds carry lyndry_order_id with no kind marker, so capturing one marks the order PAID even when the remainder was just refused, and a refused hold marks an uncollected order FAILED. The only kind guard is for deposits, which no longer exist.
  - *Decision:* Tag holds (for example lyndry_kind:'hold') and ignore them in the webhook, or drop the payment_intent handlers entirely, since every charge already writes its own status synchronously.
  - *Where:* src/routes/payments.js:255-301, src/core/billing.js:1067-1078 (hold metadata), src/core/billing.js:1270-1350 (capture, then remainder, then markFailed), .env.example:110-111 (events subscribed)
- **Charging the difference on an order that is already PAID (another bag turned up)** — The extra-bag button promises to 'Take the difference off their card' and texts that it did. chargeOrder refuses to touch any PAID order and still reports ok, so nothing is charged. The obvious fix (charge price minus amount_paid_cents) would double-charge orders paid before the ledger existed (14 Sep), where amount_paid_cents is 0.
  - *Decision:* How should 'owed' be computed on a PAID order: ledger-based, or last charged amount from Stripe? Should extra-bag use a dedicated charge-the-difference function rather than chargeOrder?
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:1258-1264, 5797-5813, 5829, 5846, src/core/extra-bag.js:219, 249-251, src/core/billing.js:559-562
- **Lyn may not book, but save_details still books** — CANNOT_BOOK withholds create_order, check_slot and set_pickup_schedule, but save_details (still offered) accepts pickup_date and calls createOrder() directly. That places a $25 hold and can text a /pay link.
  - *Decision:* Remove pickup_date from save_details (and the createOrder call), or route saveDetails through the CANNOT_BOOK refusal.
  - *Where:* src/core/brain.js:59, 1544 (CANNOT_BOOK), src/core/brain.js:358-364 (save_details.pickup_date), src/core/actions.js:895-905 (saveDetails -> createOrder), src/core/actions.js:1137-1140 (gate by tool name only)
- **'Charged once, never twice' vs two charges** — Lyn and the code comments promise a single charge. The consent text and settleTotal take the hold and then charge the remainder as a separate PaymentIntent: two statement lines whenever the total is above the hold.
  - *Decision:* Pick the customer-facing description of hold plus remainder and make Lyn's prompt match the consent text.
  - *Where:* src/core/brain.js:576, 580, 593 ('THE CARD IS CHARGED ONCE ... never twice'; 'comes off the real total at the door'), src/core/billing.js:521-524 ('charged exactly once, at the scale, for the whole amount'), src/core/billing.js:74-78, 94-97 (consent: hold taken first, anything over charged at the s…
- **Money copy and consent describe the van even under the courier model** — Customer copy says 'we weigh your laundry at your door' and 'charged at the door'. Under DYNAMIC nobody of ours is at the door: the laundromat weighs, and a refused remainder creates a payment hold rather than leaving the bags. The consent text is the legally important one. No money copy is model-aware; site.js only switches serviceArea.

- **How big is the hold?** — The amount actually held is holdFor() = max($25, 2 x the dearest Uber leg), which couples the van hold to the courier band table. Every sentence naming the hold (consent, refused-hold texts, account error, admin card, Lyn) uses the $25 floor.
  - *Decision:* Should the van model's hold follow Uber bands at all? And should every message read the actual held amount (holdFor or order.authorized_cents)?
  - *Where:* src/core/billing.js:945-948; src/core/quote.js:191-202, src/core/billing.js:86 (consent), src/core/booking.js:1670; src/routes/account.js:2943; src/routes/admin.js:1059; src/core/brain.js:580
- **Texting a card link to website customers** — cardDestination says a website customer is sent back to their account, not texted a payment link (anti-phishing and 10DLC). card-chase texts setupLinkMessage, which always contains a /pay link, to abandoned web checkouts (booking intents are online-only).
  - *Decision:* Should the abandoned-checkout chase send the account address rather than a link?
  - *Where:* src/core/billing.js:131-185 (the door rule), src/core/card-chase.js:98-146 (intent chase via setupLinkMessage), src/core/billing.js:343-356 (setupLinkMessage always mints a link)
- **Should a Stripe error count as the customer's card refusing?** — The rule is that only a genuine refusal leaves bags on a step or marks a hold refused. But stripe.js converts every error into ok:false, and billing reads that as a decline. That covers bad or mismatched keys, 'No such PaymentMethod' and network failures. #2073's hold was recorded as refused this way.
  - *Decision:* Count only card errors (StripeCardError or a decline_code) as refusals, and route everything else to couldNotCharge?
  - *Where:* src/core/fulfilment.js:1802-1816; src/routes/admin.js:5281-5284 (the rule), src/providers/payments/stripe.js:238-254, 311-321 (catch-all), src/core/billing.js:713-743, 1097-1113 (every !ok becomes declined or refused), scripts/book-francisco.js:11-17 (#2073)
- **Who may waive or charge from the JSON API** — On the web, money decisions are Admin-only (orders.override). The JSON /ops/charge and /ops/waive accept any ops session cookie or the machine key with no role check, and /ops/waive is the only waive lever in the app.
  - *Decision:* Add an Admin-gated waive button and retire the JSON levers, or role-check them.
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:5227-5229, 5407-5410 (Admin only), src/core/admin-auth.js:321-337 (requireAdminApi accepts any session), src/routes/ops.js:198-275
- **'card' vs 'payment method'** — The account pages and updateCardText say 'payment method'. The texts, buttons and pages say 'card' ('we need a card on file', 'Update your card', 'Save a card', 'Card saved'). Stripe only accepts cards now (payment_method_types ['card']).
  - *Decision:* Choose one customer-facing term.
  - *Where:* src/routes/account.js:940, 2179; src/core/billing.js:411-421, src/core/billing.js:347; src/core/payment-chase.js:96; src/web/account-setup.js:306; src/routes/account.js:3066-3069; src/core/card-saved.js:120,172,186,299, src/providers/payments/stripe.js:96, 154
- **Keep or delete the parked inline card field** — Dead since 8 Sep, but explicitly kept 'for the day that trade looks different'.
  - *Decision:* Delete (git keeps it) or keep parked.
  - *Where:* src/routes/account.js:2156-2165, src/core/billing.js:301-334; src/providers/payments/stripe.js:122-170; src/routes/payments.js:244-250

Booking ignores that rate completely: orders.create snapshots the flat config rate ($2.00 / $1.80), recurring.js does the same, and the /account wizard, the Stripe consent text, Lyn's THE FACTS, the home/faq/how-it-works/terms/town pages, schema.org and llms.txt all state the flat rates. ### schema
- **What weight LYNDRY pays the laundromat on** — The code records one rule and the reports and the manual screen use another. Rule 1: partnerBillFor() bills on the laundromat's own weight, higher or lower than ours, and the settle paths write that into orders.partner_bill_lb; migration 0050 states the same rule. Rule 2: reports.js pays on the lower of the two scales, quoting Neil as 'invoice the lowest of the two'. The manual settle form pre-fills 'Pay the laundromat on' with the lower figure. Nothing reads partner_bill_lb, so the stored figu…
  - *Decision:* Is the laundromat paid on their weight (within tolerance) or on the lower of the two scales? And should reports and invoices read the stored partner_bill_lb or recompute it?
  - *Where:* src/core/partners.js:126-140, src/core/fulfilment.js:1206, :1237-1239, :1444-1446, supabase/migrations/0050_weight_reconciliation.sql:10-13 and the orders.partner_bill_lb column comment (~:66-70), src/core/reports.js:83-97, src/routes/admin.js:1166
- **Whether orders store the courier delivery fee and courier cost** — Migration 0107 says delivery_fee_cents and courier_cost_cents stay as genuinely new facts. The code says the opposite: the delivery fee can never be known when the pickup is booked, so the hold is derived from the band table, and courier cost is a second copy of courier_deliveries.fee_cents, summed from the legs. Nothing writes either column, and tests assert delivery_fee_cents is ignored.
  - *Decision:* Before dev merges to main: should the customer's quoted delivery fee be snapshotted on the order, and so written, or should both columns stay out of production?
  - *Where:* supabase/migrations/0107_drop_duplicate_hold_amount.sql:28-30, supabase/migrations/0105_delivery_fee_and_held_amount.sql:25, :39, src/core/billing.js:934-946, src/core/quote.js:170-186, src/core/courier-legs.js:499-515, test/courier-fee-is-real.test.js:158, :216
- **Booking by text is off, but Lyn's prompt still drives booking** — CANNOT_BOOK withholds create_order, check_slot and set_pickup_schedule from the model. The prompt still injects customers.pending_pickup with instructions to 'Pass this day and time to create_order' or 'call check_slot again'. Several Lyn replies also promise to book by text.
  - *Decision:* Is booking by text permanently off? If yes, retire the pending_pickup prompt line and state, and reword the replies to point to /account. If not, keep the machinery and re-offer the tools.

- **Lockers: shelved, yet still an AI tool and a status path** — CLAUDE.md says lockers are shelved and that open_locker stays and refuses politely. The tool is still offered to the model on every message, its reply promises to book a pickup (which Lyn cannot), and ASSIGNED and DEPOSITED remain legal order statuses with tables, columns and seed data behind them.
  - *Decision:* Are lockers permanently off? If so, remove the tool and statuses in code and schedule a later drop migration for lockers, buildings and the locker columns. If not, keep them shelved as they are.
  - *Where:* CLAUDE.md:5316-5319, src/core/brain.js:245-257, src/core/actions.js:489-506, src/core/orders.js:31-33, :51, supabase/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql:18-23, :73-86, :104, scripts/seed.js:86-131
- **How many clips a van carries** — The clip inventory reads one config number, while the vehicles table has a per-van clip_count that only the seed script writes and nothing reads. Van capacity has the same split: the planner reads vehicles, but with no row the check is simply skipped.
  - *Decision:* Are van capacity and clip count per-vehicle data (then give vehicles a screen and read clip_count) or global config (then retire the vehicles table later)?

The consent names showUpCents() ($25), while holds are placed at billing.holdFor(), which is quote.holdCents() (max of $25 and the dearest pair of courier legs) in bo…
  - *Decision:* Approve courier-model wording for the card agreement and card screens before launch, and decide whether the authorised hold amount in the consent should follow holdFor().
  - *Where:* src/core/billing.js:86-101, src/core/billing.js:910-912, src/core/billing.js:945-948, test/card-page-wording.test.js:24-27, test/card-page-wording.test.js:46-51, test/card-page-wording.test.js:68-84, test/partner-portal-admin.test.js:138-150
- **Channels that still invite booking by text** — Several paths still ask customers to book by text. Ops intake buttons text 'What day would work for your next pickup?'. The followups early nudge exists to chase stalled text setups. The Google Ads message asset's starter text is 'Hi, I'd like to book a laundry pickup.'. google-tag counts sms: taps as lead conversions. All of this contradicts the new rule that orders are placed online.
  - *Decision:* Which of these stay (ops still asking by text, then booking by phone), which change to point at the online order, and should the Google Ads message asset be rewritten?
  - *Where:* src/core/intake.js:153-183, src/core/intake.js:327-331, src/core/followups.js:186-203, src/core/ad-conversions.js:127, test/google-tag.test.js:191-201, src/core/brain.js:488-490
- **Consent source falls back to a hero form that no longer exists** — /start records any post without from=popup as consent source WEB_HERO. The hero form was removed today and the popup is the only form that posts to /start, so any other post is now labelled with a form that does not exist.
  - *Decision:* What consent source should an unrecognised /start post get now: refuse it, or record it as unknown?
  - *Where:* src/routes/web.js:1464-1486, test/consent.test.js:36-47

### van-driver
- **A van clip means 'this load was weighed and matched'** — The written rule is weigh, then check, then clip: clips go on the returning bags only if the total passes the handover check, so 'a clipped bag is a verified bag'. The driver's run gives every returning bag a clip and marks it loaded the moment it is weighed. That happens before the load total exists, and still happens after the check fails. The run then stops the driver with a red 'Do not leave' card instead.
  - *Decision:* Is a clip still proof of a passed check? If yes, the run must hold back clips until the whole load passes. If no, the red block is the gate, and the weigh-check-clip rule should be retired from CLAUDE.md, journey.js and tags.js.
  - *Where:* CLAUDE.md:1383-1389, src/core/tags.js:540-557, src/web/journey.js:437-444, src/routes/admin.js:6529-6583, src/core/loadout.js:241-263, src/core/run.js:895-919, src/web/run-page.js:882-931
- **Who ends a stop: the last bag, or the driver's button** — The rule, found twice, is that the last bag never ends the stop and the driver presses a button to move on. At the delivery door, the last clip-off tap switches the card to the photo step on its own, so the 'Everything is prepped' button is disabled whenever it can be seen. At the laundromat drop, the last bag handed over moves the order to AT_PARTNER automatically.
  - *Decision:* Are these two automatic advances accepted exceptions? If so, delete door-done and amend the rule. Otherwise the door and drop stages should wait for a confirmation that is actually stored.
  - *Where:* CLAUDE.md:3464-3468, src/core/run.js:949, src/web/run-page.js:1239,1544,1572-1578, src/routes/admin.js:6286-6321, src/routes/admin.js:6792-6841
- **One implementation per job: collecting finished work from a laundromat** — The rule is that the run never becomes a second way to change an order. Today there are three implementations of 'collect the finished bags', each with different checks. (1) The run: tick stickers, weigh each bag, clip regardless, check against the customer weight only, raise an issue. (2) The order page: /return into tags.collectFromPartner, with count plus total weight, a gate before clipping and an admin override. (3) The orphaned /ops/loadout walk, which clips with no gate, reconciles again…
  - *Decision:* Which flow is the one way to collect finished work, and which weight comparisons apply to it: customer only, or partner as well? The other two should be deleted.
  - *Where:* CLAUDE.md:3384-3388, src/core/run.js:27-31, src/routes/admin.js:6151,6633,6491, src/routes/admin.js:7925-8022, src/core/tags.js:562-648, src/routes/admin.js:7308-7517, src/core/loadout.js:222-428
- **Is the order page a record or a second front door?** — Neil's call was that the order page no longer does the work, because two front doors onto one job drift. The order console (from 14 Sep) renders the next run task as a working button, and those buttons have drifted. It can never finish a pickup: it has no 'finish' or canFinish handling, and the open 'Another bag?' slot always comes before Finish. It can never mark an order delivered: the 'scan' task reads scan.allScanned, which allBagsScanned never returns. It also shows ?done= slugs raw and dr…
  - *Decision:* Should the order page offer step buttons? If it is a record, remove the task buttons and keep only the admin overrides. If it is a full second front door, fix finish and deliver and route it through the same code as the run.
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:753-766, src/routes/admin.js:3417-3437, src/web/order-console.js:482-508, src/web/order-console.js:582-622, src/core/run.js:94,125-129,318-341, src/core/loadout.js:538-546, src/routes/admin.js:3270-3274
- **Driver routes under the courier model (in-house legs)** — Neil can set either leg of an order to an in-house DRIVER under the courier model, and the commit that added this says the run and the routing board must ask carriers.js. They don't. dispatch.board, run.forDriver and drivers.board never read the carrier. /ops/routing is refused outright under the courier model. orders.create auto-assigns a driver to every order in both models, while /ops/run, /ops/clips and /ops/loadout stay open. Under the courier model, courier orders would appear on a driver…
  - *Decision:* Under the courier model, does a driver's route exist for in-house legs? If yes, filter board and run stops with carriers.isDriver(order, leg) and allow Routing when any leg is DRIVER. If no, gate /ops/run and the related screens by model and remove the in-house option.
  - *Where:* src/core/carriers.js:21-80, src/routes/admin.js:5934-6000, src/routes/admin.js:1749-1754, src/routes/admin.js:414,6039,6019,7396, src/core/dispatch.js:1124-1886, src/core/orders.js:316-317
- **Do courier orders carry LYNDRY bag tags?** — The reason given for hiding /ops/labels under the courier model is that bag tags are applied at the laundromat now. No courier-model code binds, reads or prints bag tags: the portal, partner weigh-in and courier legs never touch bag_labels. Hiding /ops/labels also removes the only way to mint and print tags.
  - *Decision:* If courier orders get tags, tag printing has to survive the courier model and the portal has to bind tags. If they don't, correct the comment and treat bag tags as van and old-laundromat only.
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:1730-1734, src/routes/admin.js:8185,8457,8534, src/routes/shop.js, src/core/partner-weighin.js, src/core/courier-legs.js
- **Which rule picks the laundromat for an order** — Every booking in both models calls dispatch.savePlannedPartner, which picks intended_partner_id by van per-mile driving cost, capacity and hours. Under the courier model the customer's price and serviceability come from courier leg prices plus the wash rate (quote.chooseFor on /quote), which can point at a different shop.
  - *Decision:* Under the courier model, should intended_partner_id come from quote.chooseFor, or does the van chooser stay the source of truth for planning?
  - *Where:* src/core/booking.js:1466-1482, src/core/dispatch.js:647-1022, src/routes/web.js:132, src/core/serviceable.js:217-224, src/core/quote.js:225
- **One hard-coded van base vs a configurable service base** — CLAUDE.md says the single hard-coded start point was removed in favour of driver bases and a settable service base, and /ops/routing/base saves one. The 'can we take this one' quote on the same Routing page, and loadout.buildRun, still start and end at geocode.BASE. geocode.js says it is deliberately one constant because there is one van. The board and the quote on one screen can therefore disagree.
  - *Decision:* Keep the Routing quote box and re-base it on the service or driver base, or drop the quote.
  - *Where:* CLAUDE.md:3486-3498, src/routes/admin.js:7274-7298, src/core/drivers.js:85-102, src/core/geocode.js:377-380, src/core/dispatch.js:442-460,1895-1904, src/core/loadout.js:468
- **'REMOVE' vs 'gated, not deleted' for the five van screens** — On 26 Sep Neil asked to remove /ops/routing, /ops/labels, /ops/economics, /ops/planner and /ops/weights. The commit deliberately disagreed and only gated them by model, because production runs the van. All five are still in production's menus. Routing, labels and weights are working van tooling. Economics and planner are calculators that read no data.
  - *Decision:* Delete /ops/economics and /ops/planner now (about 1,700 lines, nothing depends on them), or keep them until the switch. Confirm that routing, labels and weights stay for the production van until the courier model goes live.
  - *Where:* src/routes/admin.js:1724-1754, src/routes/admin.js:426,523-524,536, src/web/prelaunch-page.js:243-257
